# UE vignette shot 67c51f5 @1791371796
# Line 1 names the commit this was measured on, as the Unity verdict does.
# THE STREET IS BUILT FROM production/specs/vignette-pieces.json AND NOTHING ELSE.
#   Nothing here is authored: every position, size and rotation came out of that
#   file, which Ledger.Core wrote from the shared scene json. Untextured on
#   purpose: materials, the props and the HDRI are Phase C and Phase D.
# frameMedianMs: MEDIAN of 24 engine frame deltas after 8 discarded warm-up
#   frames, in milliseconds. NOT the Unity host's number even though both are a
#   median of 24 after 8: Unity times one Camera.Render plus GL.Flush and this
#   times a whole engine frame. frameStat on each line names which one it is.
# shotMeanLuma: mean over EVERY pixel of the committed file, 0 to 1,
#   luma=(0.299R+0.587G+0.114B)/255, the same weights the Unity sim uses.
# shotNonBlackPct: percent of shotPixels with any channel above zero.
# shotDistinctBuckets: distinct 5-bit-per-channel colour buckets, of 32768.
# a shot status of WROTE needs a decoded file with more than one bucket and
#   at least one non-black pixel. BLANK, UNDECODABLE and NO-FILE are the three
#   ways it fails, and the step exits non-zero for all three with the evidence
#   still committed.
# QUEUE 059, THE TWO MEASUREMENTS RUN 17 DID NOT HAVE. Its lantern count read
#   four of four and answered `were four lights created`, while both night
#   frames were black; its mean luma read 0.5030 over a day frame whose ground
#   plane was entirely clipped. Neither number could see it. These can:
# shotClipHiAny/shotClipHiAll/shotClipLoAll: COUNTS of pixels at the top and
#   bottom of the 8-bit range over shotPixels, never a mean. shotLumaBands is
#   eight equal luma bands, band 0 darkest: a printed series, not a bound.
# band.skyTop / band.skyCentre / band.ground: THREE GEOMETRIC BANDS of THIS
#   frame, named for what they COVER and not for what is in them. skyTop is
#   the top eighth full width and carries roofline as well as sky; skyCentre
#   is the middle fifth of it; ground is the bottom fifth. Each ships its own
#   pixel count as its denominator and its own rectangle in pixels, and a
#   rectangle covering no pixel reads NOTHING-MEASURED rather than dark.
#   meanRGB is printed because a sky and a fog inscattering colour are told
#   apart by CHANNEL ORDER: before the sky landed, the day far field read
#   249.5/250.0/250.5 and the night one 188.4/179.6/179.3, which are the day
#   and night fog colours and not a sky. A printed series, not a bound.
# bandGroundOverSky: one ratio of two measured means. A ground LIT by a sky
#   and a ground MIRRORING one both raise it; the ground band's own spread is
#   what separates them, because a mirror adds variation and a lamp does not.
# skyModel / ambientModel: READ BACK off the components after the write. Both
#   were hardcoded literals in a format string until 2026-09-09 and one of
#   them was false. skyWrites=N/of=M is write-on-change: M is how many times
#   ApplyCondition ran and N how many times the sky was rewritten, and N<M is
#   the point rather than a fault. skyHdriBoundAs names WHAT THE SKY BOUND
#   TO: the approved photograph as a long-lat PNG on an unlit dome, or NOTHING
#   and the reason. Those are different readings and one may never print the
#   other. The atmosphere and the sky light are unchanged and still light it.
# shotSunIntensityAsked / shotSkyIntensityAsked: A1(c), 2026-09-09. THE
#   VALUE THE CONDITION ROW ASKED FOR, beside the one the component read
#   back, with shotCellAgrees per frame and cellAgree=N/of=M once per run.
#   Run 38 printed five rungs that all read sky 1.000 and one control row at
#   0.350 and never printed the cross, so the cell that mattered had never
#   been rendered and nothing said so. The repeat shot is excluded from the
#   run tally, because it re-photographs shot 0 and would make the
#   denominator larger than the shot list.
# shotExposurePin*: QUEUE 235, 2026-09-10. WHAT EXPOSURE THIS FRAME WAS
#   ASKED TO HOLD, BESIDE WHAT THE CAMERA COMPONENT READ BACK, PER SHOT. The
#   rate snap of 2026-09-09 did not settle the rig: at speed 10000 the run on
#   83dec33 still read rigDeterminism=DIFFERS with 921600 of 921600 pixels
#   changed between one camera's first frame and its own repeat. The next lever
#   is the VALUE: AutoExposureMinBrightness equal to MaxBrightness leaves the
#   histogram nothing to move. asked comes from the CONDITION, exposure_pin in
#   the shared file, where 0.0 asks for nothing and is what every condition
#   older than this change carries, so AUTO on a row is not a failure. HELD is
#   the game thread's agreement and NOT a pixel's: a value written through a
#   pointer reads back off that pointer whatever the render proxy did, which is
#   why the row's own frame luma rides the same line. The separator is one part
#   in a thousand and is NOT a measured tolerance: a float round trip is of
#   order 1e-7 and a pin that never landed reads back as the engine default.
# ladder.pin*: THE SERIES THE PINNED VALUE IS SET FROM, AND IT IS NOT SET IN
#   THIS RUN. The value cannot be computed from anything committed: shotMeanLuma
#   is the mean of a tonemapped 8-bit frame and the pin is a scene-luminance
#   input read before the tonemap, with no arithmetic joining them, and the
#   adapted exposure is a render-thread quantity this process never reads. So
#   four rungs bracket the engine's own default clamp range and EVERY RUNG IS
#   PHOTOGRAPHED TWICE, once after a night frame and once after a day frame,
#   because the fault under test is a frame coming out blown because the one
#   before it was dark. afterDay and afterNight name the frame photographed
#   IMMEDIATELY BEFORE the row, read off the shot loop and never off the row's
#   name. The pairing prints as a signed DIFFERENCE, never a ratio, because
#   this tonemap is monotone and not linear. ladderSmallestDiffPin NAMES the
#   smallest and calls nothing agreement: a bound on that difference has not
#   been measured, so ladderVerdict stays SERIES-ONLY and a later commit sets
#   the number. Both clip counts ride each rung with their denominators,
#   because a mean cannot see a blown frame and a value chosen on the mean
#   alone would crush or blow one end of the run.
# expPin*: the run's tally, with the cost of the pin on the line. A PINNED
#   FRAME CAN NEVER JUDGE AN ADAPTATION MOMENT, walking out of a dark alley
#   being the example, and expPinConstantSet=no says this run printed the
#   series and chose nothing from it.
# lightAim*: A6, 2026-09-09. EVERY DIRECTIONAL LIGHT'S ASKED ROTATION BESIDE
#   THE ONE ITS COMPONENT READS BACK, with the signed residual per axis to
#   four decimals. The spec has asked for a sun at pitch -36.0 since it was
#   written and this rig rendered -82.0 on every run: the arithmetic was
#   tested, the format string was tested, and nothing tested that the number
#   reached the light. lightAimStatus=AGREES is the only passing word and the
#   step refuses on anything else WITH THIS FILE STILL COMMITTED. The bound
#   is 1.0 degree and it is NOT a measured tolerance: it separates the 46.0
#   fault from a float round trip of order 1e-4. A residual printed between
#   0.001 and 1.0 is what a real bound would then be read off.
# light lines: one per probed light, the SAME camera, condition and frame
#   counts as its shot with that one light switched off. deltaMeanFull is the
#   whole frame, deltaMeanPeak is the named grid cell in peakRegion, and both
#   halves of every difference are printed beside it (meanOn.. and meanOff..).
# THE CONTROL LINE TOGGLES NOTHING and is this run's own noise floor. A
#   lantern whose histogram does not clear the control's did not light the
#   frame. No threshold is set here: read the series, set the bound after.
# NO BOUND IN THIS RUN. lampGain and fogGain are unchanged first values and
#   the picture is not to be fixed before it is measured.
# PHASE C, MATERIALS. One surface line per surface the shared file asked for,
#   with what each map LOADED AS rather than what its filename claims, and the
#   candidates tried for every map that is not there. The base material is a
#   BUILD PRODUCT made by tools/ue/make_base_material.py in the cook step: no
#   human opens the editor, which is D1 measurement (a) in one line.
#   materialBase reads MISSING when the cook did not carry the asset, and
#   every surface below is then untextured however many maps decoded.
# PHASE C, THE READBACK. Each surface line carries what the material
#   instance answered when asked for the texture and the two tiling
#   scalars straight back, and the materials line carries the run's
#   totals over the surfaces a parameter was actually set on. It is the
#   GAME thread's copy: a value that lands there and never reaches the
#   render proxy still reads back as the same pointer.
# PHASE C, THE CONTROL QUADS. Three planes of one size at one distance
#   in front of the control camera (cam_B, ruled 2026-09-21), off the same base material,
#   carrying no street data. The first binds a 2x2 texture built in code
#   from four saturated colours, with no file and no decode; the other
#   two bind no texture at all and differ only in their tiling scalars.
#   Four colours on the first means a texture override reaches the
#   sampler. Two different cell counts on the other two means the scalar
#   overrides reach the shader. THE FRAME IS WHAT ANSWERS, not a count:
#   the quad lines say only where to look and what was asked for.
#   The controls occupy their printed boxes, so any whole-frame statistic
#   taken from this run includes them and must exclude those boxes first.
# QUEUE 208, THE CAMERA. shotCamId and the asked-against-read pose ride
#   EACH SHOT LINE, because the camera a frame was taken from is a
#   per-sample fact. The one-per-run shotCam line keeps only what is true
#   of the run: that a camera actor exists, and the map it is in. It used
#   to carry the pose, last-wins, and tools/frame-shadow-probe.py refused
#   every frame whose camera was not the last one the run placed.
# THE RIG LINE, rigDeterminism, IS THIS RUN ASKING WHETHER IT CAN BE
# QUEUE 384: THERE IS NOW ONE rigDeterminism LINE PER REPEATED SHOT, the
#   first shot and the first NIGHT shot, each naming its rigRepeatFamily,
#   and one rigRepeats line saying which shots were repeated and how many
#   agreed over a denominator. rigRepeatsIdentical keeps the zero epsilon;
#   rigRepeatsWithinBound is the capture loop's own stopping number read
#   back over the run, which is that one number used twice and not a
#   second measurement.
# QUEUE 384: shotSettle* ON EVERY SHOT LINE, AND ONE settle LINE AT THE
#   FOOT. The shutter no longer fires on a fixed frame count and hopes:
#   it takes the picture again until two successive takes agree within
#   settleBound of whole-frame mean luma, up to settleTakesMax, and the
#   committed file is the LAST take. shotSettleSeries is every take of
#   that shot in order. CAP-BIT is a frame nobody may compare to another
#   frame. The bound is a PEAK of observed converged residuals, read off
#   run 54's twelve night controls (0.00405 worst converged against
#   0.20487 smallest fault), and no gate reads it.
#   COMPARED AT ALL. The first shot's camera and condition are
#   photographed again as the last thing the run does, to a scratch file
#   that is not committed, and rigDiffPixels is the count of pixels that
#   differ over the whole frame. There is no epsilon: identical inputs
#   must be the same picture, so the honest bound is zero. Anything else
#   means a cross-shot luma comparison in this run is partly a comparison
#   of the rig. The eye adaptation SPEEDS are the one thing this probe
#   overrides, named on the tonemap line; the exposure VALUE is still the
#   engine's own, so a still is still the level a player would settle at.
# QUEUE 223, THE THIRTY PIECES NOTHING PAINTED. Until this run a piece
#   whose surface did not resolve to a pack file got no material instance
#   at all and rendered the engine default: ten card decals, ten multiply
#   decals, six shop interiors and four runs of yellow road paint. Four
#   routes now paint a piece and the census is on the materials line:
#   piecesPainted, piecesUnpainted, paintRoutes and paintUnpaintedWhy,
#   over the pieces the loop EXAMINED and not over what the file asked
#   for. pack is the twelve surfaces the pack answers for; tint is
#   interior and paint_yellow, built in code from the SurfaceSpec tint
#   because one is ProceduralOnly in the Unity host and the other has no
#   pack file; decal-card is the piece's own picture, cropped at decode.
#   THE INTERIOR WEARS THE WINDOW'S NORMAL AND ROUGHNESS, which is the
#   Unity host's own rule, and a borrowed map is named on the surface
#   line and counted APART from a found one: mapsFound still means this
#   surface's own candidate answered.
# THE MULTIPLY DECALS ARE HIDDEN AND SAY SO. A stain is a material blend
#   mode, not an instance parameter, and this build ships one opaque base
#   material, so the grime cannot be drawn here yet. Hiding is the Unity
#   host's own rule for a decal whose image does not load, and it is the
#   lesser of the two wrongs: ten grey rectangles in the carriageway are
#   louder in a judged frame than ten absent stains. The pair differs by
#   the grime until that material exists, and decalsMultiplyNote says so.
# THE CARD DECALS ARE CROPPED AT DECODE AND NOT BY AN ST PAIR. The base
#   material has tiling scalars and no uv offset, so the rectangle the
#   asset string carries is cut out of the decoded buffer instead. The
#   crop's v is measured from the BOTTOM and image rows arrive top down;
#   that flip is in CropPixels, which g++ runs here before dispatch, and
#   decalRowOrder on every decal line names which way round it went.
#   WHAT NO NUMBER HERE CAN SEE is whether the engine's plane carries the
#   same uv winding as the Unity quad, so a lettered fascia could arrive
#   mirrored. The frame is what answers that, and the answer is one
#   character in SurfaceBind.h if it is wrong.
# NO COMMENT IN THIS HEADER WRITES A KEY WITH AN EQUALS AND A VALUE.
#   Run 19 spelled this key out with MISSING beside it up here and
#   measured it as loaded down there, which tools/verdict-dupkeys.py
#   reads as one key with two values in one run. Every reader here
#   greps, and one of them takes the FIRST match. Keys are named in
#   prose above and measured below, never both.
